## Current priority — encrypted CircleCI restore candidate

[**New one-time encrypted CircleCI transfer specification**](PR32-INTEGRATION2-CIRCLECI-ENCRYPTED-TRANSFER-DESIGN-2026-10-30.md) is **design-only** and based on confirmed [CircleCI config](https://github.com/ccpcerp/ccpcerp/blob/061e65710eb4b527fc8ac579aaab8ccad4dd131d/.circleci/config.yml) and [existing combined test job](https://github.com/ccpcerp/ccpcerp/blob/061e65710eb4b527fc8ac579aaab8ccad4dd131d/.circleci/four_reports_stage.sh). It proposes:

1. Preserve exact original Integration2 snapshot; encrypt a streamed four-artifact plus SHA256 manifest `tar` into **age ciphertext** with new, single-use X25519 recipient generated on trusted owner-controlled device. Local decrypt-verify, no plaintext upload and no ciphertext committed to Git or CircleCI artifacts.
2. Use an **already available owner-controlled private temporary object store** if one is confirmed; one random object, least-privilege short-lived read URL/credential, deletion and verification. **No existing S3/R2/other object store has been established**. Don't create a paid service by assumption. Its bearer URL and the age private identity must be protected in a **new restricted CircleCI context**, never in config/pipeline parameters, and expression must deny `job.ssh.enabled`.
3. Prepare a **separate manual/default-OFF CircleCI restore job** in a new reviewed GitHub app branch. Use exact reviewed CI script source identity but install the CCPC app **at original backup SHA `0c223d7...`**, not PR32 schema. Distinct disposable sidecar MariaDB/Redis/site `ci-integration2-restore.localhost`, strict no legacy DEV/PROD credentials/mounts, no outbound email/hooks, no data artifacts or secret logs.
4. Run only upon **separate exact-scope owner approval** for confidential data egress, new context/storage, GitHub CI code creation and one bounded CI-credit run. Verify actual four-file import, unique database, two synthetic Companies, app/patch metadata, and representative public/private attachments. Record any missing fixture files honestly. Delete/revoke temporary data/key after authorized completion.

**Provider reality:** CircleCI's pipeline parameters are not secret and current CircleCI context docs allow restrictions by project, branch and `not job.ssh.enabled`. Secrets masking does not secure artifacts, redirected logs or SSH debug. Existing Build #52 tests are not proof of actual archived-backup recovery.

GitHub combined checks independently confirm `ci/circleci: integration2-restore-manual-approval` **success**, `ci/circleci: integration2-backup-restore-isolated` **failure**, https://app.circleci.com/workflow/936272f1-3675-4177-8146-ee0180d204bd/job/680dcc8d-b5c2-424e-8b2d-bfa74220e83d . This is **not a new SSH prompt hang** (fixed); the original pinned app Git SHA was first fetched successfully from the existing HTTPS authenticated checkout but failed to transfer **locally** into `apps/ccpcerp` from that checkout. The local command's stderr was intentionally suppressed to avoid leaking auth/URL, so the exact runtime Git stderr cause is **not directly observed**. The earlier B2 prefetch and prerequisites must have exited successfully for the sequential step 105 to execute, but **the uploaded log has no independent exact ciphertext size/hash output**. The final age decrypt/MariaDB restore **did not run**. Nothing touched DEVELOPMENT or PRODUCTION.

**Reproduced likely root cause without credentials/network:** CircleCI's checkout is a **blobless partial/promisor clone**. In an isolated disposable local Git experiment, a plain `git fetch --depth 1 origin <older SHA>` retrieved the old commit but left its blobs promised. Transferring that SHA via a local repository `git fetch -c fetch.fsckObjects=true <blobless checkout> <older SHA>` failed because local upload-pack cannot lazily fetch missing objects; adding `--no-filter` to the authenticated source fetch retrieved complete pinned blobs and allowed local copy, exact SHA checkout, `git fsck --full --strict --no-reflogs` and content check to PASS. This proves the class of failure and repair in a toy repo, not the exact provider stderr or a live CircleCI success.

**Implemented, verified in GitHub:** On `ccpcerp/ccpcerp:review/pr32-integration2-isolated-restore-2026-10-30`, HEAD now `1402644e7ed9e38ff843b6893b82af433aa1abe9` (two reviewed commits ahead of `3da594e...`; **only** `.circleci/pr32_restore_stage.sh` and `.circleci/test_ci_restore_config_static.py`). Existing authenticated HTTPS fetch now includes `--no-filter` to hydrate the exact source revision before transferring locally; retains noninteractive timeouts/redacted error, exact ref and strict full-fsck/clean-worktree validation. Added a **real file:// local Git integration unittest** that constructs a filtered blobless checkout, fetches the historical revision using `--no-filter`, copies it locally into a second checkout, checks SHA, fsck and tracked data; no external network, backup, DB or credentials. Static test now requires `--no-filter`. An equivalent disposable toy reproduction and successful no-filter transfer were run in this assistant session; **new GitHub HEAD has no CircleCI statuses yet**. **Identified test-harness bug from exact GitHub source:** In committed artifact `scripts/development/operator/ccpc_exec_http_logged_one_shot_20261102.sh`, the helper is:

```python
def denied(reply):
    if reply.status_code in (401, 403, 405, 417):
        return True
    if reply.status_code == 200:
        return False
    try:
        data = reply.json()
    except ValueError:
        return False
    return 'PermissionError' in str(data.get('exc_type', ''))
```

Pinned public `frappe/frappe` source at `988e54f3c4c291e2077a83809663f123731abe76` has `frappe/exceptions.py: class CSRFTokenError(Exception): http_status_code = 400`; `frappe/auth.py:HTTPRequest.validate_csrf_token()` throws `frappe.CSRFTokenError` on a mismatched/missing token when none of its exceptions apply. Consequently a **correctly rejected HTTP 400 CSRFTokenError** would be incorrectly classified as NOT DENIED by this test helper, producing exactly the user-visible `POST WITHOUT CSRF ACCEPTED` assertion message. **But since the test did not record `response.status_code`, `exc_type` or exception safely, we cannot infer it was 400 rather than HTTP 200/another status.** Avoid claiming an actual CSRF bypass or successful negative check; need a future request to record status and safe `exc_type` and assert `status=400 AND exc_type='CSRFTokenError'` when appropriate. `400` alone is not sufficient because it can represent unrelated client errors. `frappe.conf.ignore_csrf` was checked false in test preflight (and passed). Check gateway response validation separately and do not bypass security or use API keys to mask the issue.

**Future gates:** Current version consumed, do **not rerun** or reenable the new user. Only after separate owner authorization, a fresh one-time corrected and logged test with a new test identity could exercise 400/CSRFTokenError correctly and continue to four authenticated POST positives and four-company isolation.
